People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

It sounds really clear, like someone is standing right next to me.

I just… I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it, but it’s been hard to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something, rather than just saying not to do something?\n\nPatient: Mostly it tells me not to do things… like not to touch certain cars, or not to trust my husband. I don’t think it’s, um, ordering me exactly? It just sounds really clear, like a man right there, coming from the vents or under the hood. Sorry, I know that sounds strange.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something specific?\n\nPatient: Um… not like “go do this right now,” I don’t think. It’s more specific in the other direction, like it says not to touch certain cars, or not to trust my husband. And it’s so clear, like a man standing next to me, even though it sounds like it’s coming from the vents or under the hood. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you that you must do something?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it tells me I must do something, exactly. It’s more like… it warns me, I guess? Like not to trust my husband or not to touch certain cars. It feels very direct because it’s so clear, like a man right there, from the vents or under the hood, but I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you that you have to do something, or that something bad will happen if you don't?\n\nPatient: Not really “you have to,” no. It’s more like it warns me not to do things… like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. I don’t think it says what will happen exactly, it just feels like something bad could, because it’s so clear—like a man right there, coming from the garage vents or sometimes under the hood. I’m sorry, I’m trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever give you commands or instructions that you feel you must follow?\n\nPatient: I mean… not commands like it’s ordering me around, exactly. It’s more that the man’s voice tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. And when it’s that clear—like it’s coming from the garage vents or under the hood, like someone standing right next to me—it’s hard not to listen to it. I know that sounds bad, I’m just trying to be accurate.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is your own thoughts or does it sound like it's coming from outside your mind?\n\nPatient: It doesn’t feel like my own thoughts, no. I mean, I wish I could say it was just in my head, but it sounds like it’s coming from outside me—like from the garage vents, or sometimes under the hood of a car. It’s a man’s voice, really clear, like somebody standing right next to me. I know that probably sounds strange… but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever say anything mean or critical about you?\n\nPatient: Not really about me, no… not like calling me names or saying I’m a bad person. It’s more warning-type stuff, like telling me not to trust my husband or not to touch certain cars. The voice is really clear, though, like it’s coming from the garage vents or under the hood, like a man standing right there. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or upset when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get anxious. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but when I hear that man’s voice that clearly—like from the garage vents or under a hood—it really shakes me. Especially when it’s telling me not to trust my husband or not to touch certain cars. I know it probably sounds odd, but in the moment it feels so real that I get upset and kind of on edge.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I guess there’s always some possibility, right? I’m trying to be fair about it. I know it sounds far-fetched, and I don’t want to seem like I’m just refusing to listen.

But honestly… I’m still pretty convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track me. Even when they show me the footage and say there’s nothing there, it doesn’t really make me feel settled. I wish it did. So… maybe the voice could be mistaken, but it doesn’t feel mistaken to me.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’d like to say yes, just to sound… I don’t know, more reasonable. And I do try to question it.

But honestly, when the voice warns me, it usually feels like it’s picking up on something real. I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me the footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really feel reassured.

So… maybe it could be wrong sometimes, but it doesn’t feel wrong to me when it happens.

I mean… I’d like to think it would. That’s probably the right answer, right? If someone showed me real proof, I should be able to accept it.

It’s a man’s voice, really clear, like somebody standing right next to me. I know that probably sounds strange… but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever say anything mean or critical about you?\n\nPatient: Not really about me, no… not like calling me names or saying I’m a bad person. The voice is really clear, though, like it’s coming from the garage vents or under the hood, like a man standing right there. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or upset when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get anxious. It’ll say things like not to trust my husband or not to touch certain cars. I know that probably sounds strange… but that’s what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: How long does the voice typically last when you hear it?\n\nPatient: Usually it’s not very long… maybe just a few moments at a time? I know that probably sounds strange, but it’s not like it goes on all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the warnings from the voice are based on real events or possibilities?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know how it sounds, and I don’t want to seem unreasonable. I know that sounds far-fetched. It’ll say things like not to trust my husband or not to touch certain cars. I know that probably sounds strange, but it’s not like it goes on all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the warnings from the voice are based on real events or possibilities?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know how it sounds, and I don’t want to seem unreasonable. I’m still pretty convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep track of what I’m doing… even when they show me the footage and say there’s nothing there. I know that sounds far-fetched. Even when they show me the footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really feel reassured.\n\nSo… maybe it could be wrong sometimes, but it doesn’t feel wrong to me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else would agree that your husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and security cameras to track you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if most people would agree with me. The voice is really clear, though, like it’s coming from the garage vents or under the hood, like a man standing right there. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or upset when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get anxious. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but when I hear that man’s voice that clearly—like from the garage vents or under a hood—it really shakes me. Especially when it’s telling me not to trust my husband or not to touch certain cars. I know it probably sounds odd, but in the moment it feels so real that I get upset and kind of on edge.\n\nDoctor: Is the voice something you hear all the time or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes… it’s not every second or anything. Mostly it happens at the garage, like I’ll hear that man’s voice from the vents or sometimes under the hood, clear like someone’s right beside me. I know that sounds like a lot, but it’s not constant… just when it happens, it feels really real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see, smell, or feel things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: Not really seeing or smelling things, no… at least not that I’ve noticed. It’s mostly the hearing part, the man’s voice from the garage vents or sometimes under the hood, clear like someone standing right next to me. It’ll say things like not to trust my husband or not to touch certain cars. I know that probably sounds strange… but that’s what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: How long does the voice typically last when you hear it?\n\nPatient: Usually it’s not very long… maybe just a few moments at a time? It’ll be clear, like somebody’s standing next to me, coming from the vents or under the hood, and then it kind of stops. I know that probably sounds strange, but it’s not like it goes on all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the warnings from the voice are based on real events or possibilities?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know how it sounds, and I don’t want to seem unreasonable. But yeah, I do think the warnings are based on something real, or at least real possibilities. I’m still pretty convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep track of what I’m doing… even when they show me the footage and say there’s nothing there. I know that sounds far-fetched. I just can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is trying to tell you the truth about your husband and the things it warns you about?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want to say it like I’m completely sure, because I know that probably doesn’t sound good. But yes, part of me does think the voice is trying to warn me about something real. Like with my husband and the shop owner… I’m still convinced they’ve been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing, even when they show me footage and say nothing’s there. I know it sounds far-fetched. I just can’t really let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the voice might be mistaken about the things it's warning you about?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess there’s always some possibility, right? I’m trying to be fair about it. I know it sounds far-fetched, and I don’t want to seem like I’m just refusing to listen.\n\nBut honestly… I’m still pretty convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track me. Even when they show me the footage and say there’s nothing there, it doesn’t really make me feel settled. I wish it did. So… maybe the voice could be mistaken, but it doesn’t feel mistaken to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is ever wrong about the warnings it gives you?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, just to sound… I don’t know, more reasonable. And I do try to question it.\n\nBut honestly, when the voice warns me, it usually feels like it’s picking up on something real. I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me the footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really feel reassured.\n\nSo… maybe it could be wrong sometimes, but it doesn’t feel wrong to me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else would agree that your husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and security cameras to track you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if most people would agree with me. Probably not, if I’m being honest. I know it sounds far-fetched, and I know when I say it out loud it makes me sound… not great.\n\nBut I’m still convinced something is going on with my husband and the shop owner, like they’re using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep track of what I’m doing. Even when they show me the footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t feel like that proves it’s not happening. I wish I could just accept that, but I can’t really shake it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband and the shop owner tracking you would change if you were shown proof that it's not happening?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d like to think it would. That’s probably the right answer, right? If someone showed me real proof, I should be able to accept it.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, when they show me the camera footage and say there’s nothing there, it doesn’t really change what I think. I know it sounds far-fetched, and I don’t want to seem unreasonable, but I’m still pretty convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing.\n\nSo… I guess proof might help a little, but I don’t know if it would make the feeling go away.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband and the shop owner tracking you is based on facts or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I want to say it’s based on facts, because that sounds more… solid, I guess. And it doesn’t feel like just a feeling when it’s happening.\n\nBut I know some of it probably looks like I’m connecting things that other people don’t see. I know it sounds far-fetched. I’m still convinced, though, that my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really feel like that proves it’s not happening.\n\nSo… I guess it’s partly a feeling, but to me it feels like facts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever change your mind about your husband and the shop owner tracking you?\n\nPatient: I… I want to say yes, because I know that’s what sounds reasonable. I don’t want to be someone who can’t change her mind.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know. Even when they show me the footage and say nothing is there, I still feel convinced that my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing. I know it sounds far-fetched, I do. I just… I can’t seem to let it go completely.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
